                          I       •                J               I                         •

               (8) The object moved at least 20 times in all directions and
               the movements "Were very quick. It was seen south-west of the
               observer and very high up. This object was seen to move in all
               directionsJ up, down, away and then qiickly return to the origiilal
               position .
                                           .
               2.     The on call member of tnis detachment made a patrol upon
               receipt of this information, however the object had travelled a
               great distance to the south-west by this time and could only be
               made out to be multi-colored lights.        .
                      There were no Qther reports made to this . detachment r egarding
               any •imiw sightinga .    ReliabiMty or the witnesses is unknown.
               CONCLUDEQ ~~

                                     Wayne ERICKSOR ---- Sighting of U.F.O .                               Appendix fl
                                     Kinuao, Alberta --- 21 SEP 76
                             '


                 On tho 2lat ~of September, 1976, at approximately 5:15 P.M.,
          I was in my room at t he Kodiak LOdge' Hotel, 1<inuao, Alta. I was
          looking out the window towards the south when I not~ced an object
          flying ' in a Westerly diredt~on. This object appeared flat and round
          with a hump in the centre, m~ch l~e a fried egg. The object did not
        • ;ppear to have any wings. It was flying at about 6000 feet and at a
          sp~ed of approxinlately 150-200 miles per ))our. This ob~ect changed
          directions abruptly, more of a fluttering motion. It did this very
          fast and always maintained a Westerly direction. '
                 It appeared silver coloured at first and then as it proceeded
                                                                                           .
                                                  •
          Wost, it changed to a white. It was travelling towards•.the direction
          of the sun . This vehicle may havo goon going in a more south-westerly
                                       \                                  .
          direction than west. I only saw the one object:• There was no-one else
          present with me and ~o-one ,else saw it that I know of. There was no
             ...
          no.ise and it appeared close enough that i£ it was an airplane there
          would have been noise , I have a co~ercia~ pilots license fxDc 9286
          and am familiar with most types of airplanes. I judged the size speed
                         '
          and altitude with                                                   '
                              reference to a CESNA 402. That •s, it appeared to   be
           •                  I                               ~           .
          about the same s.izp aa a CESNA 402 would be at 6000 feet . The object
                                I                                               .
          appeared ' to be about two miles away when I first seen it. I maintained
          a visual contact with it for apptoximately one minute. , I had not been
          drinking prior to this sighting . I am part-owner of the Hotel and I
          ~a preparing to paint .                               I•

                     l.       Further to our message #67, Mrs. Myrrla JOLIVIT of 10 Lyla
                     Drive, LaWrencetown , was contacted on 4 OCT 76 and she advised that   ·
                     on the 23 §EP 7~ at approximately 9:00PM, Atlantic Daylight Time ,
                     she eaw~a very right l igqt in the sky looking south from her residence
                     over the Atlantic Ocean . JOLIVIT r eported the sky to be clear at the
                     time with no stare. The object seen is described as peing flat 'on the
                     top with rounded sides and a bright firey bottom. The object ascended
                     from the sky to the ocean on three (3) separat~ occasions. After                                                1


                     about ten (10) minutes it had ascended and continued out of sight .
                     The object l~ft no t rail as it ascended an~ made no sound.
                                                     I                                                                       0

                     2.       Mrs .• Mary COVE Apt. 813, Stoney Brook Manor, Halifax, N. s.
                     waa at the JOLIVIT resiAence on the 23 SEP 76 , and confirmed ·the
                     sighting on the above date and time. COVE could offer no other
                     informatioq regarding this object • .

               Gordon BEATTIE is very interested in planes and other things
               military. He . is definite 1:t was not a plane. fie is very familiar
               with all types of craft flying out of CFB Sbearwater.. His parents

               but are since. Parents also checked papers for Saturday, Friday

               and w4s advised only ~ planes were flying around at that time.
               One. plane from CFB Greenwood to Shearwater1 o~er plane from
               Bermuda to Halifax International Airport. UPO was travelling
               in a Northerly direction and just disappeared. Message 164
               forwarded to National Research Council , Meteor Center, Ottawa .
